24

Pricing kinds, the price following its Contract until held, and who may change it

Five pricing kinds priced by one function: a price follows its Contract (the range in force, the rate or discount as they stand) until a typed price, a prepayment invoice or AUTHORISED holds it, else 'Needs a price'.

Its dependencies (21a, 23) are done; it is next in run order. Take branch cu/23-17a into master first: 24 builds the price precedence around 23's allocation in feeFor.

- The release stays per payment through today's Run payables. The weekly ISO-week payment cycle (US-10.2.7, OQ-47 Proposed) is Phase 39a's: no copy in this phase names a payment day, a week number or a weekly cycle.
- BCTIs are counted in exactly one place: bctisFor (pure, in src/domain/billing/bcti.ts, with tests) over bctiRecords (one selector, one record per procedure ACCPAY, the pre-payment invoice's included, with its stored anaesthetist, issue date and its receivable's paid date). One BCTI per receivable invoice, each counted once against the anaesthetist who did the procedure, and only once its receivable is paid in full, in the month it is paid (so no paid BCTI is missed or charged twice). The paid-only rule is one switch (BCTI_COUNT_RULE.paidOnly); one comment beside the function and the Admin caption are the only places that say the count is being confirmed with AA's accountant (OQ-60, OQ-29). Later feeders are 22, 36, 38b, 39 and 39b. No other code counts BCTIs, including the demo trigger's disabled check.
- The monthly run raises one AA-FEE invoice per active anaesthetist per month (fixed items plus per-BCTI charge times the count), refuses a future month, and is a no-op on a rerun. The acceptance example must reproduce: $500 + $5 x 40 = $700 before GST.
- The AA fee invoice is its own case in billing.aaFeeInvoices, outside billing.cases, with a kind:'aaFee' ACCREC against the anaesthetist's existing Xero contact and no ACCPAY, raised through one exported raiseAnaesthetistInvoiceInto path, the one path for an invoice from AA to an anaesthetist (Phase 36 gives it its ledger pair; nothing is built for OQ-71's negative with no later payment, which is handled outside the system). It is always a separate invoice paid into AA's own account and never netted: no code path deducts it from a payable, a payables run or a disbursement (test it). It must never reach payables, GST activity, Overdue, the monitor pipeline, the Payment received webhook triggers (hide them on a fee pair with a `when`), the reconciliation poll or the archive job, and a fee payment creates no BillingReceipt.
- InvoiceNumber and Reference are stored on both Xero records, not derived at view time; the ACCPAY number keeps the -P suffix; the ACCREC's paidAtISO is stamped once by the completing payment; no NHI crosses to Xero.
- Every write goes through mutate() with an audit entry naming the right actor (office button, scheduled system run, Xero payment, settings save). Time comes from the demo clock only.
- Demo triggers go through the Phase 14 registry, shown only on their screen, surfaces bar, with disabled states, and bodies in src/store or src/shared: "Run scheduled month-end fee run" and "Seed a month of BCTIs" (tops Dr Rutherford up to exactly 40 receivable invoices paid in full in July 2026 and disbursed, with service dates 1, 2, 3 and 6 July, before the canvas horizon start, so no added List collides with a generated canvas List; the $700 example then reproduces under the paid-only count) on Admin AA fee invoices, and "Record fee payment" on a fee pair in the Xero simulation. "Run monthly fee invoices" is a product office button on the AA fee invoices screen, not a demo trigger. The re-homed Payment received Full and Half triggers now release exactly the amount received and are hidden on a fee pair; Phase 14's PWA pwa-payment-full / pwa-payment-half on Mobile · Balances share that body, so they release exactly too and lose any fee wording. The registry (src/shared/demoTriggers/) may import only from src/store and src/domain, so seed-scoped entries name ANAE constants and the screen publishes its month through useDemoTriggerContext. Nothing is added to the Control Panel page. No new PWA entry is needed; confirm none of this phase's new entries show in the PWA sheet.
- Do not re-litigate the PROGRESS.md Decisions log, except the 2026-07-29 fee ruling this phase supersedes.
- Follow the design files: teal is the only action colour, crimson stays identity only, pills use semantic tokens, amounts are mono with tabular-nums. No en or em dashes in any app copy.

Phase goal: the canvas becomes Slots and Lists. Every active anaesthetist has an AM and a PM Slot per day from their start date to the end of a configurable horizon (four months), written free and then painted with their calendar and the surgeons' recurring bookings, with no invented sessions; the status on the Slot is the one mechanism, with no record reconciled into Lists. A List is created only when the office assigns one (or a recurring booking projects it) and then shows in place of the status. The displayed status never depends on Bookings and matches in all three apps and the finders, added anaesthetists appear on the Admin Day grid, default times and the horizon come from one setting, Reassign moves a List between Slots intact with no prepayment re-check, the office's finder lists the available anaesthetists by priority tier with not-preferred pairings apart through Phase 17's helper (never in the anaesthetist apps), every new List is ACTIVE, and the UI never says "slot".

- Mock backend only. Every write goes through a store action and the audited mutate(), with before/after metas and demo-clock timestamps. Components never own domain state. setAvailabilityRange, clearAvailability, createAvailabilitySeries and deleteAvailabilitySeries are each one mutate(), all or nothing on refusal, with one audit entry plus one entry per List affected.
- One mechanism (OQ-27 and OQ-64 answered): the calendar writes the Slot's own status directly. A series is a rule (weekdays, sessions, every 1 to 4 weeks, start, optional until) plus exceptions; it writes its Slots, and a single-instance edit or delete is stored as an exception so no later series write, clock roll forward or horizon extend overwrites it. Nothing reads availability from anywhere but Slots. A series fills only Slots at the default status and in no other series, and reports the rest as left as they are. One helper, seriesCalendarFor, feeds active series (with their exceptions) into 28's generateCanvasForDates as calendar input on every generating path (rollCanvasForward, setHorizonMonths when extending, an earlier start date or a reactivation), so they are painted with the anaesthetist's calendar BEFORE recurring bookings (OQ-81 part 1, settled). A recurring booking on a series-closed session is then never placed: 28's generator returns it as a RecurringClash, which Phase 31 turns into a Draft List. The generator's closed test reads isClosed over the status records; it still draws no RNG. Series rules live in src/domain/availabilitySeries.ts, pure and tested.
- The Slot status set becomes a user-maintained master list (masters.slotStatuses, US-01.2.2), availability values only. Each status has a fixed internal ID (SlotStatusKey) that rules read, never its label; seeded IDs are 28's stored values (free, holiday, unavailable), and an added status gets a generated ST#### ID, shown read only and never edited. Label, long label, description, colour (one of the six design tokens) and treatment are editable, plus "open for bookings" and "anaesthetists can set". Seed the owner's starting set: Free (default, dashed), Holiday (long label "On holiday") and Unavailable (hatched), with today's colours. No "available for emergency" and no extra kinds of unavailable seeded; an admin can add them (Greg: several kinds of unavailable, same effect). A rename or recolour shows in every view with every rule unchanged, and an added status works with no code change (US-01.2.2's two criteria; test both). No provisional line anywhere.
- The List types (private, public, pre-op) stay a fixed List attribute with their own tokens, so a session holding a List shows the List in its place with the design's List colours; no status ID can equal a List kind. Log on the "For the owner's review" list that the catalogue has no List kind.
- Availability is not the List's state (US-01.2.2 since 2026-10-07): the status master, the display key, the legends and the filters never hold DRAFT, ACTIVE, SUBMITTED or AUTHORISED. ListState and its chip stay on the List, untouched. validateSlotStatusDraft refuses a label or long label that is a List state or its label (plus "Draft" and "Draft List", ready for Phase 31), the Availability statuses header says this is a session's availability, not a List's state (Draft, Active, Submitted, Authorised), and the table marks rows "In use" or "Retired", never "Active". A recurring booking is a pattern of Lists, not a patient Booking: never shorten it to "booking" in copy.
- A conflict is one derived rule: a List conflicts when its hospital is closed on its date or its Slot's status isClosed. The pure rule lives in src/domain/conflicts.ts, and mutate() reconciles every List whose facts a mutation touched. No store action writes List.conflicts itself. Remove every per-path stamp (29's availabilityClash and every Slot-status path that calls it, including setAvailability, setAvailabilityRange, clearAvailability, the series actions and the roll-forward series write; 28's closedHospitalConflict in placeListOnSlot; addHospitalHoliday; the move filter; the generator; applyPhase06Conflicts). Warn inline before confirm and never disable it. 28's slotNotAvailable refusal goes, and moveListToSlot stops refusing a closed target (it still refuses an occupied one); the reassign picker keeps open Slots first, grouped and ordered by 17's pairing-preference helper (never "blacklist" in copy), and adds a collapsed "Not available" group.
- List states: an assigned List is ACTIVE (15b), a DRAFT List is a Draft List (Phase 31). The projection creates ACTIVE Lists, updates and removes only its own empty ACTIVE Lists, and never touches a DRAFT, SUBMITTED or AUTHORISED List. Never label an assigned List "DRAFT" in copy.
- A conflict is only what the catalogue keeps at 60e2d1e (unchanged since 3d3a18c): a List at a closed hospital, or a List (and its Bookings) in a session already marked unavailable. A recurring booking landing on an unavailable session is not a conflict: the projection and generator skip it and report it as skipped, and Phase 31 turns it into a Draft List. An anaesthetist marking a booked session unavailable is return-or-assign (US-01.5.5, Phase 32): build no prompt and no Draft List conversion here; until 32 it is flagged as a named interim, through 29's per-Slot write helper, the one place 32 hooks in. A sickness the office records (the anaesthetist phones in) keeps the List and its Bookings and is a conflict: that is the one provisional reading (OQ-81 part 3), kept on the trigger and the seed and on the "For the owner's review" list.
- Never put the word "slot" in app copy (OQ-64): say session, AM or PM. Slot stays a code word. Status words come from Phase 29's status master labels; rules key on the fixed status ID.
- A conflict clears when its clash goes (holiday deleted or moved, hospital changed, Slot marked available, List reassigned to an available anaesthetist) or when the office clears it. An office clear holds only while its cause stands, needs no reason, and can be undone with "Flag again".
- The List changes colour with the design's amber attention treatment (semantic.warning tint and border plus the "!"), keeping the kind's left bar and label. No new status colour and no new hex. Crimson and teal never mark a conflict.
- Recurring bookings create their Lists across the horizon before any Bookings exist: the roll-forward paints the new far-edge day, after the anaesthetist's calendar (OQ-81 part 1). Changes reach the existing canvas through "Apply to canvas now": the sheet's primary button saves and projects in one action, with a live preview line from the same pure plan, and a view-level "Apply to canvas now" re-paints every active recurring booking onto sessions that have come free (it never removes). Both project from tomorrow to the far end of Phase 28's horizon setting and never touch a List with Bookings, a SUBMITTED or AUTHORISED List, or an office-edited List. Weekend patterns and start and end dates work, and a recurring booking can be retired (no hard delete). Requiring hospital and surgeon at save is Phase 31's pairing rule. The projection is pure, with Vitest tests, and agrees with the generator.

- The DRAFT state. ListState becomes 'DRAFT' | 'ACTIVE' | 'SUBMITTED' | 'AUTHORISED', and List is a union on it: AssignedList (ACTIVE, SUBMITTED or AUTHORISED, with an anaesthetist and a Slot) and DraftList (DRAFT, no anaesthetist, no Slot, a draft trail). LIST_STATE_LABELS gains DRAFT, shown only on a Draft List beside "Unassigned"; no assigned List reads "DRAFT" or "Open". Only an ACTIVE List can be completed and submitted; submitting or authorising a Draft List refuses with "Assign an anaesthetist first.". A store-wide invariant test holds FT-07.1: every ACTIVE, SUBMITTED or AUTHORISED List has its anaesthetist, Slot, day, session, hospital and surgeon, and every DRAFT List has neither anaesthetist nor Slot.
- A Draft List is a List record, plus a draft trail (origin, source, since, by, and for a recurring clash the day it stands for). There is no separate collection, so its Bookings hang off its List id and assignment keeps the id.
  - createDraftList and the recurring clash write through one insertDraftList and leave schedule.slots and every other List untouched; prove it with a test.
  - Only assignDraftList takes DRAFT to ACTIVE, through attachListToSlot, in the session its day and session fix (the admin chooses only the anaesthetist), in one commit with a draftList.assign meta (before state DRAFT, after ACTIVE); the draft trail is kept with assignedAtISO. 15a's warnings are derived on read (store/warnings.ts), so they need no call, but their rules and OpenWarningRow must tolerate a Booking on a Draft List.
  - An ACTIVE List only becomes a Draft List through detachListToDraft, built and unit-tested here for Phase 32 (origins movedToOffice and unavailableReturn); it refuses a SUBMITTED or AUTHORISED List, and no screen in this phase calls it.
  - removeDraftList soft-removes and refuses while it holds an active Booking; redateDraftList moves it and its Bookings to another day and keeps its waiting time.
  - A Draft List cannot be submitted, authorised or billed, and an anaesthetist cannot add a Booking to one.
  - No projection, roll forward or new-anaesthetist path may overwrite a List id a Draft List holds, and a recurring booking never projects a second List or Draft List for a day it already answered for (the Draft List keeps recurringBookingId, and a recurring clash keeps the day in draft.projectedFor even after a re-date or a removal).
- Prepayment (US-06.3.5, US-06.5.4, D20). A Booking on a Draft List has no anaesthetist, so 27's routine generates and withdraws nothing for it. Built default, logged for the owner: the first assignment of a Booking that never had an anaesthetist is its setup, so assignDraftList runs 27's routine (syncPrepayment) once for it with the listAssigned cause 27's plan exports for this call (use the name 27 shipped); reassigning a List that already had an anaesthetist (one Phase 32 returned), re-dating and detaching re-trigger nothing (honour system; the payee repoint is Phase 41's, D38).
- The recurring clash (OQ-81 part 2, built as the answer): one pure recurringClashDraft turns each clash (Phase 28's generator RecurringClash or Phase 30's skipped entry: a recurring booking covering a closed session) into a DRAFT Draft List with the recurring booking's hospital, surgeon, kind and notes, origin recurringClash and a stable derived id. It runs on every path that yields clashes: addRecurringBooking, editRecurringBooking, applyRecurringBookingsToCanvas ("Apply to canvas now"), every roll forward, the horizon extend, an earlier start date, and the seed's generation. Confirm 29 paints series onto new Slots before recurring bookings (seriesCalendarFor in the generator's calendar step), so no recurring booking is ever left flagged on the anaesthetist; if any path still places and then flags, fix it (work item 9). Never for a past day. It writes no availability conflict and never shows on Conflicts for the anaesthetist (US-01.5.4 AC3); a hospital closure still raises Phase 30's holiday conflict. An edit, retire or move of the recurring booking updates or removes an empty clash Draft List and keeps one with Bookings. Phase 30's sheet wording for skipped sessions now says they go to Draft Lists.
- Phase 17's helper, privately (US-01.3.5, US-01.3.6, D44). The assign picker is draftListAvailability (free, not available, occupied; no preference knowledge) ranked once by 17's rankAnaesthetistCandidates and split into Free, "Not preferred with Ms Reid", Not available and Already has a List (disabled), each tier-ordered and shuffled within a tier (requestKey from the List id and suggestionSeq), with 17's tier filter, Preferred pills and an Everyone / Free only toggle. assignDraftList writes list.pairingAcknowledged (through notPreferredEntryIds) only for a not-preferred pairing. Add store/draftListActions.ts to the allowlist in 17's officePrivacy.test.ts as an office write path; it must not import store/officePrivate.ts, so the PWA closure check stays green. The PWA stand-in skips a not-preferred pairing through draftListFitsAnaesthetist, a boolean, and never names a preference or a tier. No inline preference checks or sorts anywhere.
- Warn, never block, except for the real guards:
  - A closed session (Unavailable, Holiday or any closed status) or a not-preferred choice is selectable, warned before save, and assigned. A closed session gets a conflict through Phase 30's path and its warning tells the office to check with the anaesthetist (US-01.4.7); a not-preferred pairing gets 17's acknowledgement entry.
  - Only these refuse: an occupied session, a missing hospital, surgeon, day or session, a passed day, removing a Draft List with active Bookings, detaching a List that is not ACTIVE, and the office-only guard.
  - A Draft List at a hospital with a holiday carries Phase 30's holiday conflict like any List, and shows on the Conflicts screen as "Unassigned".
- One pairing rule. It lives in the pure pairingIssues helper (domain/listPairing.ts, never confused with 17's pairingPreferences.ts), and every write path calls it. List.hospitalId and List.surgeonId become required, and List.anaesthetistId and slotId are present exactly on AssignedList. The seed changes are:
  - the Fitzgerald Tue 21 PM "Surgeon TBC" List goes and her session is left empty; the seed gains three request Draft Lists in state DRAFT (St George's with Mr S. Tan; Forte Health with Mr V. Nand holding two Bookings, each Procedure with its source wording, a procedure, a Contract and the patient as payer, on no prepaid set; Christchurch Eye Surgery with Ms A. Reid as the long wait);
  - the seed's generation makes the recurring-clash Draft Lists from today on (12 at plan time: Ngata Tue 21 AM, Beaumont's and Ngatai's leave, Ropata in August, Sharma in September; recompute and pin the count);
  - Phase 30's seeded Rutherford sickness stays exactly as Phase 30 left it;
  - the pre-op Lists sit at the new AA rooms location, which is never a Contract holder;
  - every pre-op and public List (all 16 null-surgeon recurring bookings plus the RNG acute branch) names a surgeon who holds no Contract, sits in no holder group and has no active pairing preference;
  - every backdrop List gets a hospital.
  Remove every "AA rooms", "Not assigned" and "Surgeon TBC" fallback, and every "Unassigned" that is not the Draft List flag.
- Determinism first:
  - The acute surgeon comes from its own slotRng stream, so the fill stream and every Booking are unchanged.
  - Regenerate Phase 28's golden fixture, and show that the diff contains only the stated changes.
  - No fee, invoice or Contract resolution may move. A pre-op Booking's Contract is held on its Procedure (Phase 20), and its picker's candidates are unchanged at AA rooms (no hospital-holder Contract fits there); update Phase 20's picker test. The new seeded Bookings touch no existing figure.
  - Seed timestamps are constants; canned request days and the staged clash day derive from the clock.
  - Bump PERSIST_VERSION by one and extend the migrate test.
- Time waiting comes from the demo clock through one helper. It moves with every clock change, freezes at assignment or removal, and survives a date change.
- Order: every Draft List view sorts through one compareDraftLists, ascending date (US-01.6.2, soonest first), then AM before PM, then the longer wait, then id.
- Vocabulary: every Draft List word ("Draft List", "Unassigned", "DRAFT", the origin labels) comes from shared/scheduleTerms.ts, every pairing and tier word from 17's PAIRING_LABELS and TIER_LABELS; "Unassigned" flags every Draft List surface; no new app copy says "slot", "blacklist" or "whitelist".
- The Day dashboard gains a Draft Lists card in the right rail (under 15a's To-do card, designed with it, leaving the place Phase 32's notification pool takes), a Draft Lists band above the grid rows for the viewed day, a booking count on every List block (cancelled Bookings excluded), and "N Draft Lists" in the header summary. The Admin nav gains a Draft Lists page (the one date-sorted place) with an amber count badge. The List drawer gains an unassigned mode (the DRAFT chip and "Unassigned") with "Available that session" (free, clear anaesthetists in tier order, US-01.6.3), the Bookings section, Add Booking, Assign, Edit, Change date, Remove and History; after assignment it shows ACTIVE.
- Demo triggers go in the shared registry, with bodies in src/store. Nothing is added to the Control Panel page. They are:

Phase 28's stand-in stays beside it.
  Adding a recurring booking for an anaesthetist on leave, Add Booking, Assign, Change date and Remove are product actions, not triggers.

Phase goal: a List with no anaesthetist is a Draft List, the first state (DRAFT) of a List's lifecycle. Each has a hospital, surgeon, day and session, all required, may already hold Bookings, and takes no Slot. Draft Lists are flagged "Unassigned" with their waiting time and sorted soonest first, beside the day in the right rail, in a band on the Day dashboard, on their own Admin page and on a nav badge. Only the office assigns one, by choosing the anaesthetist from everyone potentially available, grouped and tier-ordered by Phase 17's helper with soft warnings and a full audit, and it becomes ACTIVE; or the office removes it or changes its date. A recurring booking that lands on a session the anaesthetist has already marked unavailable becomes a Draft List. Every List has exactly one hospital and one surgeon, and the Day dashboard shows each block's booking count.
32Anaesthetist moves their own List, and the notification poolDone 15 October 2026

An anaesthetist returns their own List to the office (it goes back to DRAFT) or moves it into a colleague's free session, at once, with no preference warning, also when they mark a booked session unavailable.

After 29 and 31. 2 sessions. Schedule track. Questions: OQ-79, OQ-84.

Phase plan

Add a deep-equal test on a prepaid Booking. The payee repoint is Phase 41's (OQ-80's default, D38): leave a one-line comment at list.ownerMove in the core and build nothing for it. The same move is the doer rule: whoever submits a List did every procedure on it. FT-01.4 closes.

Screens a catch-up phase rebuilds are not migrated by a U phase: each such phase (21a, 22, 22a, 23, 24, 25, 26, 27, 27a, 33, 33a, 34, 35, 36, 37, 38, 38b, 39a, 39b, 40, 42, 43, 43a) carries a work item to build on the shared components and migrate the copies on its screens, and a review line that a new private copy of a shared component is a finding (docs/ui-unification/PLAN.md section 3).

How a phase runs
  1. Get the owner decisions first. D1 to D11 (the table at the top of ROADMAP.md) each gate one phase. A phase whose answer has not come in builds the stated default and labels it provisional.
  2. Pick the next phase from the table below, respecting the order in "Sequencing and rules". The Contracts track (17 to 25) and the Schedule track (28 to 32) each run strictly in order.
  3. Open a fresh Claude Code session and paste that phase's kick-off prompt. The agent reads the roadmap, its phase doc, the gap detail for its items, the prototype maps and PROGRESS.md before touching anything.
  4. Drift check first. The catalogue keeps moving. Each session diffs its items against c9b65ad, reports what changed, and adapts the plan before building.
  5. The agent plans and builds straight on. It turns the phase doc into work-sized steps and starts building without waiting for approval, so phases can run back to back (ROADMAP.md "Owner review: agents test themselves").
  6. Every phase ends the same way: npm run build, npm run build:pwa, npx vitest run and (for UI work) npm run shots green; the checklist run and reported item by item; the adversarial review-and-fix pass (PROGRESS convention 18); the demo guide patched for any beat it broke; the catalogue screenshots brought in step with the app (the phase's capture recipes created or updated, then a full npm run capture in requirements-board/ with no failed recipe and npm run verify:board green, PROGRESS convention 19); a PROGRESS.md entry. Agents never commit.
  7. When the catalogue changes, follow README.md: re-grade the changed items, merge them into gaps.json, update the affected phase docs, and re-render this page with node requirements-board/agent/skills/update-build-plan/tools/build-index.mjs.

Demo buttons live on their screens. Anything that cannot be shown through normal use (scheduled jobs, backend events, external systems) gets a trigger in the harness bar's "Demo actions" menu, listed only on the screen where it matters. The installed PWA has no harness bar, so it gets the same list as a badged demo-actions sheet, plus office stand-ins for beats that wait on the office. Phase 14 builds the mechanism; every later phase registers into it.

The checklists are the contract. Each phase ends with a short manual test checklist. The agent runs it itself in the running app and reports each item with evidence; nothing is handed to the owner per phase. Each PROGRESS entry ends with a "For the owner's review" list, and Phase 44 gathers them into one list for a single review once every catch-up phase is done.

What each milestone can show
  • After 16 Built. Booking vocabulary everywhere, with Copy a Booking gone, photo capture out of the Add a booking chooser and an assigned List called ACTIVE. Every demo action on its own screen, in the harness bar and on a handset. Warnings, never blocks: one routine, the to-do list, a triangle on Bookings, the prepayment gate turned into a warning, no confirm step at submit. The payable equals the receivable, AA's fee is a monthly invoice, and Xero holds no patient names.
  • After 38a The office's schedule (28 to 32 built, finished by 32a and 32b): every session stored and free by default over a configurable horizon, Slots and Lists, statuses as a user-maintained list, the availability calendar, recurring bookings, Draft Lists assigned by the office with preferences and tiers and never onto an unavailable session; unavailability, sickness included, forcing a return or hand-on and never a conflict, the conflict dashboard showing hospital closures; whoever moves a List choosing what the vacated session becomes; Lists handed on and single Bookings moved into a free colleague's session as a new List, colleagues listed by availability alone; the notification pool with notices ticked resolved and alerts from a List or Booking; and the anaesthetist changing a submitted List until it is authorised, a main view from today and a search for past work. S2 is re-scripted.
  • After 25 An out-of-hours List after the PM session (32c, a working design), then the catalogue's flat Contract model end to end: Parties (no insurer master) with billing details and delivery by email or post, and Contracts as one priced item each (AA code and holder reference code, bills its holder or the payer, links, prepaid, five pricing kinds, the override on RVG kinds only, Active or Disabled, edited in place with an audit trail, never deleted) with No contract · RVG held by AA; AA's specialties and funding tags (SXAP, HNZ, ACC) on surgeons and Contracts and an open-price fallback for every invoice destination; RVG groups from the NZSA guide with the range shown and an RVG code by type-ahead; every schedule item a fixed-price Contract with backdatable price ranges, combinations as Contracts, prepaid price lists above zero, each Contract's invoice description and default RVG code, starting values from the RVG group; optional itemised modifiers, age and P1 pre-applied and removable; one Contract per Procedure through the decided step rail (where the invoice goes, Payment type or the hospital's tag, the Contract by typing under a 'No contract' heading holding the two backstops, No contract · RVG and No contract · Fixed price; 'Needs an invoice destination'; the funder's path 'Suggested', never narrowing), changeable by the anaesthetist until AUTHORISED, never onto or off prepaid; one procedure text the hospital's replaces with the original in the history, and the invoice description by its defaults, an admin's edit always winning; a payer on each Procedure (provisional on OQ-140), shown and changed in its 'Paid by' line, the change offered to the Booking's other Procedures billing that payer, a Procedure added later copying a shared payer, 'Payer: not billed yet' before a Contract, the contact rule and the child warning per Procedure, who is billed by the Contract and one invoice per payer, the insurer or funder and approval number on the Booking (a warning for insurers only); the form following the pricing kind, completion as three conditions and office review flags; invoices in the anaesthetist's name by the Party's delivery or to post, with the Split in the Procedure's 'Paid by' and a share to another person; the RVG multi-procedure rule on RVG-priced Procedures; five pricing kinds with a price following its Contract until held, a backdated range reaching only unheld Procedures, the anaesthetist seeing what the price comes to and overriding only where allowed, the office changing any price with no reason; and a lock at AUTHORISED that reproduces invoices, with each Contract's history. S1 Beat 3, S2 Beat 2, S3, S4 Beat 3 and S5 are re-scripted.

Still parked: the A and B split of a session, the pre-op consult in AA's room and the Procedure's hospital reference (DM-68, on OQ-136).

The catalogue keeps one List per Slot (US-01.1.1, Agreed) until the split is agreed, so building it would contradict an agreed rule across the grid, generation, recurring painting, availability, moves, conflicts and the finders (about 36 files read the session). Unparks into a phase after 32c once AA agrees it and says how the halves' times, a half handed to another anaesthetist and a List already holding Bookings work.

Unparks into 39b (as an event kind) or 38b (as an additional invoice) once AA describes the case. It is not the ACC pre-op assessment, which 39b builds.
  • DM-68 New data-model delta (17 November, added by the adversarial verifier): the Procedure's billingReference (the hospital's contract or approval reference, captured in three forms, prompted by the office billing setup and printed by 22 as the holder layout's cross-match hospital reference) has no catalogue home now that a Contract names no holder references and the Booking keeps only the insurer approval number (US-11.4.3). The delta itself says to hold it until OQ-136 is answered (D82, open, no recommendation): dropping the references for good means removing the field, its capture, the prompt and the cross-match hospitalRef (a PERSIST_VERSION bump); a Contract setting that asks for them means a Contract-driven Booking input instead. Unparks into the phase after OQ-136's answer (24 if still unbuilt, else a small follow-up).
About this plan

The prototype was built in July against the original RFP, in fourteen phases. The requirements catalogue has since become the source of truth and moved a long way: Bookings instead of Cards, Slots and Draft Lists, one Contract per Procedure locked at authorise, admin-decided hospital matching, an internal ledger with Xero as its mirror. This plan closes every verified gap in 58 further phases, numbered 14 to 44, each one focused session (at most two) with one coherent deliverable, and every automatic or external event given a demo button on the screen where it matters.

The prototype is the July RFP model with Phases 15 to 32 layered on. Calendar, Slots, Lists, Draft Lists, pairings, tiers, warnings engine, the AA fee run and the Xero simulation are close to the catalogue. Money and intake are not. Of roughly 235 graded items, about 40 Match, about 25 are Partial, about 15 are Missing, and the rest are Contradicts or Grouping. Contradicts dominates EP-02 to EP-08, EP-11 and EP-12: the prototype implements a different, superseded design there, not a smaller version of the new one. EP-01, EP-10 and most of EP-13 are near done. The core is one coordinated rework: Contract (holder, dated versions, lines, flags) replaces billing route plus Type 1/2/3. Pricing, completion, prepayment, invoicing, payer, review and events all hang off it. Statuses were not used to scope work; they appear below only where an open question (OQ) makes a gap unsafe.

Plan baseline c9b65ad. The full plan is ROADMAP.md, the gap analysis GAP-ANALYSIS.md, the data plan.json.

Rendered by build-index.mjs from plan.json and status.json, plan baseline c9b65ad.
